This isn't answered using Plato's equipment section: they check the text currently in force, the applicable ruling and their Intervención de Armas, taking their licence and the specific details of the gun and the location.
Validity check. For every legal claim, record the BOE identifier, the article, the text consulted and the published update date. This chapter's date only shows when the editorial review was done. If the rule changes, the text stays pending until the two versions are compared.
16.5

Sporting sources

The law, the weapons regulations, cartridges, ground authorisations and internal rules deal with different matters and must be checked within their own sphere. Sporting sources add the discipline, the edition and the competition conditions.

How do you avoid mixing the rules of Universal Trench and Olympic Trap with a club's practice or with Plato's educational choices?

To cite a sporting rule, you first need to state the discipline and the edition. FITASC published a regulation for Fosse Universelle-UT dated 1 January 2025. This document sets out five machines and five stations spaced 2.5 m apart. The shooting line is 15 m from the front of the trap. It also defines ten official schemes, a height check at ten metres and rounds of 25 clays. The same edition caps the pellet load at 28 g, with the tolerance it states, and covers guns, safety, rotation and incidents.

ISSF brings together its general and shotgun rules in the Rule Book 2026. On 2 August 2026, the official page linked to the second printing from July, in force since 1 July. It contains the Trap rules, the layout, the fifteen machines grouped in front of five stations, the ammunition, the sequence, regular or irregular targets, faults and referee decisions. Before reusing a figure from an earlier edition, it should be compared against the current one.

“No target” is a common expression in Spanish, but the actual decision depends on the regulation. A clay that comes out broken, an irregular flight path, an early release or an incident caused by the shooter don't always get the same treatment. The referee applies the rule and checks its conditions. The shooter can't decide on a re-throw just because the flight path seems difficult. In training, a clay can indeed be repeated deliberately to learn. That educational repeat is different from a regulatory re-throw.

National federations, event notices and clubs add information about categories, entry, timetables, licences and local running of events. They have authority within that scope. A single competition sheet can't be used to redefine an entire discipline. Plato doesn't certify a shooting ground either. The Universal and Olympic modes reproduce the current official tables and compare them row by row against independent test data. Robot distinguishes two regulated profiles from three other popular, non-normative models. Matching parameters doesn't turn the graphics, the ballistics or a real facility into certified material.

When two sporting documents appear to disagree, the date they took effect, the discipline, the authentic language and the scope of the competition are compared before choosing one.

Shooting ground case. A clay comes out already broken. The shooter keeps the gun pointed in a safe direction and waits for the referee's decision. Afterwards, they record the discipline, the edition and the applicable article. Plato's Repeat target button doesn't show what that decision would be: it just relaunches the last flight path without adding it to the round.
Citation rule. A sporting table must state the governing body, the full name of the event, the edition, the date it took effect, the rule number and the units. The simulator's values are compared against it in a separate table. They should never be called “official” just because they seem reasonable.
16.6

Physical and ballistic sources

A Universal rule can't validate an Olympic preset or a local round; each piece of data must stay tied to its own discipline. Claims about physics and ballistics need other kinds of evidence and greater care.

What evidence allows us to talk about velocity, gravity, wind, choke, spread and breakage without presenting a simplified model as a certified ballistics table?

Basic physics explains relationships, but it doesn't fix every figure on its own. Gravity accelerates objects downward. Air drag reduces their velocity. Wind can deflect the flight path, and a pellet cloud spreads out. However, the clay is a spinning disc that experiences aerodynamic forces. The pellets also interact with the air and with each other. That's why a parabola and a cone are useful for teaching, even though they don't fully describe the real flight.

Andert, Freudenthal and Levedag measured clays with cameras observing from several viewpoints. They then calculated parameters using an aerodynamic and motion model. Their work shows that a flight path can be reconstructed and that aerodynamics matters. It doesn't offer a universal constant valid for any clay, machine or wind. In 2024, Deng and colleagues modelled a specific 24 g load of 433 pellets out to 50 yards. They included air drag and the force that separates the pellets. It's a very useful reference for understanding the complexity of the cloud, but it doesn't directly calibrate Plato.

Pattern tests call for another precaution. Davidson and colleagues measured a shotgun's patterns. Under their conditions they found greater density with full choke than with cylinder, and they also explained the appearance of gaps and irregularities. An open 2026 study on extended chokes found a narrower spread as their length increased, in the tested configuration. None of these studies lets you predict how the reader's own gun will behave. For that, the specific combination of barrel, choke, cartridge and distance has to be tested.

Breaking the clay adds further variables: the material, the shape and the energy concentrated at the point of contact. Plato approximates the clay as a sphere and uses an internal minimum value to decide whether a pellet breaks it. That value is a rule of the model. It isn't a certified property of every clay. The manual can explain the energy of motion and contact, but it can't claim that a result from the simulator guarantees a real break.

Exercise. Keep the flight path and the moment of the shot the same, change only the virtual choke and watch the pellet cloud. The valid conclusion is comparative: the control changes the spread it implements. The invalid conclusion would be “my shotgun will have exactly this diameter at 35 m.”
Physical hierarchy. A distinction is drawn between general laws, measured values, published models, code adjustments and visual observations. Every figure must include its units, the test conditions and the material used. When no calibration exists, the trend is explained rather than promising an accuracy that hasn’t been measured.
16.7

Code-derived data

To measure velocity, spread or energy, you need to state the gun, the ammunition, the distance and the method; a single isolated result isn't enough. The code lets you check which simplification Plato uses in each case.

What exactly does the reviewed version of Plato do, and how can it be cited without confusing the code, the intent and measured reality?

The technical review was closed on 2 August 2026 against commit 14a8bf6fafa29b3b26699e8c930f78d18d0d0e5c. In that version, app.html has the SHA-256 fingerprint d1af33c6d0c7615f768c66e44ec2bb8f9341abff1f18e197207e2e2deb686858. The commit makes it possible to retrieve exactly the same files. The fingerprint makes it possible to check that they haven't changed after being downloaded. Canvas 2D draws the scene and requestAnimationFrame updates time. Plato doesn't use an external physics engine.

The check starts from these values: Universal discipline, scheme fu1, station 3 and slow motion 0.35x. The release delay is 0.03 s and the cartridge shows 400 m/s. The chokes are 3/4 and Full, the variation is 45%, the wind is 0 and the rib gain is also 0. Universal has ten tables with five rows each. Olympic has ten tables with fifteen rows. That's 50 and 150 regulatory rows respectively. Robot offers five profiles. ABT CPSA 2026 and Trap1 FITASC 2025 are presented as regulated. The other three are popular models marked as non-normative.

solveClayLaunch() builds a parabola in space. The curve must satisfy the height at 10 m and the landing distance. To do this it uses gravity reduced to 9.81 × 0.42. targetAt() adds a simple lateral offset for wind. The shot uses 78% of the value shown by the cartridge control. It also uses 306 pellet directions, an exponential velocity loss with distance, and an internal minimum of 0.55 J. CLAY_RADIUS_M is 0.055 m, which is half the ISSF nominal diameter of 110 mm. The clay is approximated as a sphere because the model doesn't simulate its orientation.

The recommended aim repeats the travel-time calculation and adjusts the horizontal and vertical angles, called yaw and pitch. To do this it uses the same core physics as the diagnostics. Repeat target copies the last release without increasing the counter or changing station. A normal round runs to 25 targets and rotates across five stations. Slow motion reduces the speed at which the simulation plays out. The visual aids keep real time so they can be read comfortably.

pelletHitTarget() doesn't check for impact only at the sampled instants. At each interval, closestPointOnRelativeSegment() calculates the shortest continuous distance between the pellet's motion and the clay's. It then works out the instant of possible contact and evaluates the distance and energy there. This avoids missing a crossing that happens between two time steps, within the linear approximation of each segment. Even so, contact is calculated using a sphere that envelops the clay. The disc's orientation, edge and real fracture aren't simulated.

scripts/verify-schemes.js, with SHA-256 a1e2e667…350946, compares 200 records row by row against independent regulatory data. That's 50 records from FITASC Universal and 150 from ISSF Olympic. scripts/verify-ballistics.js, with SHA-256 2d76d179…42d1fc, tests the centre, the edge and the outside. It also checks energy and range, chokes, the seed, slow motion, rib gain and the yellow guide line. Lastly, it checks the relationship between shots 1 and 2. scripts/verify-manual.js, with SHA-256 71d962f3…c699b8, checks the 204 units, their images and their summaries. These tests always produce the same result for the same input and check the implementation and the edition. They aren't an experimental validation of the real ballistics, machine, cartridge or breakage.

Reproducible case. Select Universal, fu1, station 3, wind 0 and 1.00x; record the three fingerprints and the controls before comparing. “I tested it yesterday” isn’t enough if the table, the browser or the code could have changed.
Source rule. Comments explain intent; the expression that’s executed determines behaviour; the test verifies a specific property. If the three disagree, an issue is opened rather than picking whichever text is more convenient.
16.8

Beginners' glossary

The hashes, tables and tests allow the review to be repeated, but they don't replace external experimental validation. The glossary now explains this technical language for beginners.

What do the words you hear on a shooting ground mean, and which ones are technical, regulatory or simply colloquial?

Action (báscula)
The central body of a break-action shotgun where the barrels and mechanisms hinge.
“Caramelo”
A colloquial expression for swelling or a mark on the face after a problematic gun mount or recoil; it isn't a medical diagnosis.
Choke
The constriction or fitting at the muzzle of the barrel that affects how the pattern opens.
Comb (carrillera o lomo)
The top area of the stock where the cheek rests. On some shotguns it can be adjusted.
Ejector (expulsor)
The mechanism that throws the fired cartridge clear when the gun is opened; an extractor only lifts it so it can be removed by hand.
Gun mount (encare)
The action of placing the gun in a repeatable way relative to the body and the line of sight.
Hold point (punto de espera)
The reference point you start from, reading the flight and beginning the movement before you see the trajectory.
Lead (adelantamiento)
The gap you see between the point of aim and the clay, needed so the pellets and the clay arrive at the same place.
Meeting point (punto de encuentro)
The future point where the clay and the pellets meet. It doesn't have to be where the clay was when the trigger was pulled.
No target (plato nulo)
A common way of referring to a target that must be re-thrown, depending on the conditions and the decision under the applicable regulation.
Pattern (plomeo)
The distribution of pellet strikes on a flat surface at a given distance and with a specific combination.
Recoil pad (cantonera)
The rear part of the stock that rests against the shoulder.
Rib (banda o solista)
The elongated strip along the top of the barrels that helps you sense alignment. It shouldn't become the main thing you look at.
Robot
A machine that oscillates or is programmed to vary the throws. The rules depend on each event.
Round (serie)
A regulated set of clays; in Universal Trench, the FITASC edition consulted sets it at 25.
Stock (culata)
The rear assembly that connects the gun to the shoulder, cheek and hands.
Swing
The continuous movement with which the gun and body follow through or track across the flight path.
Trap
The family of events in which clays are launched from machines positioned in front of the shooters. It isn't the exact name for every discipline.
Trap house (foso)
The structure in front of the stations that houses the machines in trap disciplines.
Trillo
A colloquial term noted by the author for certain local or fairground rounds; it isn't a universal regulatory category.

The glossary helps you understand, but it doesn't tell each person what to choose. “A tighter choke” describes a setting, and it doesn't mean it's the best one. In Plato, “rib gain” is the educational name for a control that raises the point of impact relative to the sight. It isn't equivalent to a universal stock measurement. If a word varies by region, discipline or manufacturer, the variant is kept and its context explained.

Exercise. Before a first round, point out the stock, recoil pad, comb, action, rib and choke on an unloaded gun, under supervision. Then sketch the hold point, flight path and meeting point without handling ammunition. Vocabulary and safety are learned together.
Terminology check. When a definition has an effect that can be checked, it must link to a regulation, a manual or a part of the program. Local words must be identified as such. Statements about the body don’t replace a health assessment.
16.9

Beginner FAQs

Using each concept correctly helps you ask questions and learn without turning a word into a recommendation that suits everyone. Beginners' most common questions apply this vocabulary to their first decisions.

What does someone need to know before turning their curiosity about clay target shooting into a safe first session?

Do I need my own shotgun, or can I try one first? Don't assume you have to buy one first. Ask a club or federation about courses and equipment that can be used legally under their procedures and supervision. Availability varies; trying different setups before buying helps you discover weight, gun mount and fit.

What permit do I need, and do I have to join a federation? To own and use your own shotgun in Spain you need the relevant administrative authorisation. The usual reference for these firearms is the E licence. The federation licence belongs to the sporting sphere. It may be required depending on the activity, insurance and organisation involved. Confirm both matters with the Guardia Civil, the federation and the ground.

How many cartridges do you get through? A regulated round has a specific number of clays. Consumption depends on how many shots the discipline allows and how many the shooter actually takes. In Universal, up to two shots per clay are allowed. So it's not a good idea to always assume 25, or always 50, cartridges. Training, repeats and incidents also change the count.

Is it normal not to hit anything at first? Yes. Seeing the clay, starting the movement, holding the gun mount, judging the lead and continuing the swing are all new tasks. A low score doesn't on its own explain the cause. Repeating a simple flight path with an instructor lets you change a single variable and get a clear correction.

How many rounds should you shoot? There's no universal figure. The session is stopped at any sign of pain, reduced control or difficulty following instructions; the coach adjusts volume and breaks.

Personal comment. I prefer short sessions when starting out, because fatigue, a drop in concentration and poorer support can lock in mistakes. This is a personal recommendation, not a universal prescription.

Why does my shoulder or face hurt? Pain should never be treated as normal. It may be related to support, gun mount, fit, cartridge, posture or too much volume, but the simulator doesn't diagnose anything. Stop, ask the instructor and seek medical attention if the discomfort is intense, persists, or there's an injury.

Why do I lose sight of the clay? It can be hidden by the barrels, the gun mount can break down when you lift your head, or your gaze can drift to the rib. Correcting this is individual and needs supervision. An adjustable stock doesn't automatically fix an inconsistent technique.

What protection do I need? Glasses and hearing protection are worn before entering the shooting area, in line with the ground's rules. FITASC requires them in its Universal competitions; NIOSH documents that impulsive gunfire noise calls for adequate protection and recommends double hearing protection for shooting.

First call. Tell the club: “I’m starting from scratch, I don’t have a gun, and I want supervised training.” Ask about paperwork, protection, equipment, cost, duration and the protocol if you need to stop partway through. That conversation stops you buying blind.
Limit. These answers are for guidance and link to the relevant authorities. They don’t grant authorisation, don’t replace a medical examination, don’t set the right amount of training, and don’t assess how well a gun fits you.
16.10

Simulator FAQ

The answers for getting started point you towards the club, supervision, protection and current procedures, but they don't replace the responsible authorities. Questions about the simulator must also separate its controls, its limits and the permissions for its use.

How should Plato be read without expecting accuracy, controls or permissions that the reviewed version doesn't offer?

Are the speeds real? The control shows metres per second, and the tables generate launch speeds. However, the model is tuned to teach visual relationships and hasn't been certified against every cartridge, machine or condition. It can be used to compare a faster, slower, longer or more angled release within the same review.

Why do I miss even when the line looks right? Impact isn't decided by a drawn line. The code moves 306 pellets and applies spread, velocity loss and energy loss. It also calculates the shortest continuous distance between each pellet segment and the clay, which is still moving. There may be a gap in the pattern, or contact with less energy than the internal minimum.

How do I repeat a clay? Once the release has finished, press Repeat target. The position, speed, machine and station of the last flight are cloned. Repeating doesn't increase the counter or rotate the station. It can't be triggered while the clay is still waiting to launch or is still in flight.

How do I change station? Use the slider or Station − and Station +. During a round, Plato rotates automatically between 1 and 5. Changing with the buttons resets the round; it's worth noting the station before comparing.

What do the chokes do? The Pattern shot 1 and Pattern shot 2 controls change the radius of the pattern the simulator creates. They don't reproduce a commercial choke exactly, and they don't replace a real pattern test.

What is rib gain? It raises the physical direction of the shot by up to 0.96° relative to the point seen at the sight. It's there to study a high impact. It doesn't automatically represent millimetres of comb height, a real pattern percentage, or a recommended adjustment.

How do I turn on slow motion? Move Slow motion below 1.00x; the starting value is 0.35x. Use 1.00x to compare against normal time. The warning disappears once that value is reached.

Can I install it or modify it? Yes, the MIT licence allows it. Keep the copyright and permission notice. For a reproducible copy, serve the folder over local HTTP and record the commit.

How do I attribute it? Keep LICENSE, “Copyright (c) 2026 Roberto Canales Mora” and the original URL https://robertocanales.com/proyectos#plato. Identifying a modification stops it being confused with the author's version.

Exercise. Repeat the same clay three times: first aim at the clay, then add lead, and finally keep the lead but change only the first choke. Record the result and avoid adjusting wind, cartridge and rib gain all at once.
Source of truth. These questions describe review 14a8bf6…, identified by the commit and the file fingerprints. The interface texts are for guidance only. The functions repeatTarget(), syncControls() and fire() determine what the program actually does.
16.11

Technical questions

To understand the interface, we need to know what this version calculates, which features it doesn't offer, and how to reproduce and attribute the project. The technical questions then help anyone who wants to review or extend it.

How is the model built, and what steps should a change follow so other people can review it?

What graphics engine does it use? The reviewed version uses the browser's Canvas 2D API and JavaScript included in app.html. requestAnimationFrame drives the loop; no Three.js, WebGL or external physics library was found.

How does it calculate the flight path? Each row states the horizontal angle, the height at 10 m and the range. solveClayLaunch() calculates the tilt and the curve of a parabola that satisfies that data. targetAt() calculates position using reduced gravity and a lateral offset for wind.

How does it represent the pattern? The radius grows with distance and changes with the choke setting. pelletDirections() distributes 306 directions using a golden angle, a seed and a variation. It also introduces small differences in speed and release. It's a cloud generated by the program, not the measurement of a real cartridge.

How does it detect impact? Each pellet advances in time steps and is compared against the moving centre of the clay. Between two positions, closestPointOnRelativeSegment() calculates the shortest continuous distance. This means contact doesn't depend on exactly matching a time sample. The impact breaks the clay if the distance doesn't exceed 0.055 m and the pellet keeps at least the internal minimum energy. The program also records the point of closest approach, to explain a miss or a weak contact.

How are the presets defined? FU_SCHEMES, ISSF_SCHEMES and ROBOT_SCHEMES are JavaScript structures. Universal contains 10 × 5 rows and Olympic 10 × 15. Robot separates ABT CPSA and Trap1 FITASC, which generate continuous angle and height within regulated limits, from three popular, non-normative models with circular, weighted or pseudo-random sequences. Its controls allow you to deliberately go outside the limits to compare.

How do you add a discipline? Adding an option to the menu isn't enough. The sporting authority, the geometry, the table and the units need to be identified. Then the selection and origin are implemented, the limits are explained, tests are added and a scenario is created. The manual, the FAQ, the sources and the image must also be updated. If the discipline isn't regulated, it must appear as educational or experimental.

How are changes tested? verify-schemes.js compares 200 rows against independent regulatory data and checks the internal physics of those throws. verify-ballistics.js checks the centre, the edge and the outside. It also checks energy, range, chokes and the seed. Slow motion, the rib, the yellow guide line and the relationship between shots 1 and 2 are added to these tests. External measurements and broad testing of interaction, browsers and responsive design are still missing. Passing both audits doesn't validate the physics through a real experiment.

How is a version documented? The commit, date, browser, tests, changed parameters, affected sources and migration notes for the user are all recorded. The changelog explains the observable consequences; the technical file keeps the formulae and the evidence.

Change case. To double the wind’s intensity, you don’t just change 0.28. You state the goal, provide a measurement, add a test with a sign and units, update the stated limitation, and compare a reproducible scene before and after.
Cross-check. The table, the function, the test, the control, the label and the text must all explain the same thing. A change is incomplete if any of them still carries the previous units or meaning.
16.12

General warnings

The engine, the formulae, the presets and the tests form a chain that must be documented every time the code changes. The general warnings are a reminder of which decisions fall outside that technical part.

What decisions should never be made using only this book or the simulator, however complete the explanation seems?

Safety. Plato doesn't teach you how to load, unload, deal with a stoppage or keep the barrel pointed in a safe direction. A screen doesn't reproduce people standing nearby, real cartridges, mechanical failure, recoil or noise. In-person training and the ground safety officer's instructions always take precedence.

Legality. This chapter provides guidance on Spanish sources, but it doesn't offer legal advice or resolve personal cases. Licensing, safekeeping, transport, purchase, cartridges and use can depend on the rule in force, the type of firearm, the holder, the quantity and the activity. Check the BOE and the Guardia Civil before acting.

Training and health. The simulator doesn't replace an instructor, gunsmith, doctor, optician or audiologist. Pain, swelling, tinnitus, hearing loss or vision problems require you to stop exposure and get a proper assessment. Another shooter's experience isn't a diagnosis or a training programme.

Model. The flight path, wind, pattern, energy and breakage all contain simplifications that the project states openly. Its values don't certify a machine, don't certify a cartridge, don't fit a stock and don't predict a score. Comparisons can still be useful even when the exact scale doesn't match reality.

Prices and availability. Any cost mentioned in the manual is an observation tied to a date. Club, region, supplier, brand, licence, travel and taxes all change. Recalculate using current sources, and don't use an old table as a contractual quote.

Changing regulations. The review from 2 August 2026 doesn't guarantee it will remain current in future. Check the ISSF edition, the FITASC regulation, BOE amendments, Guardia Civil procedures and the specific event notice. A live URL doesn't prove its content hasn't changed either.

Warranty and liability. The MIT licence provides the software “as is”, with no warranties. This legal notice doesn't remove the duty to correct errors in the text. Nor does it remove your personal responsibility to practise safely and with proper authorisation. A modification made by someone else can change the results. Whoever publishes it must identify it as such.

If a discrepancy comes up, keep the configuration, capture the result and report it along with its version. In a real, unsafe situation, don't investigate first: stop the session and alert the person in charge.

Decision traffic light. Green: compare two virtual setups. Amber: take a hypothesis to the ground with an instructor and a single variable. Red: use Plato to decide that a gun is safe, a licence is current, or a facility meets the regulations.
Risk management. Every sensitive claim must state the hazard, the user exposed to it, the competent authority, the version and the escalation action. A disclaimer of warranty doesn’t replace a design that avoids misleading people.
16.13

Manual version control

Neither the book nor the simulator replaces the law, the regulation, training, the ground safety officer, or professional judgement. For this warning to stay accurate, we need to keep proper control of versions.

How do you show which manual matches which version of the simulator, without inventing a number the project hasn't published yet?

As of the review date, no release tag or published version number was found that would let this edition be called “1.0”. So the chapter doesn't invent that name. Commit 14a8bf6fafa29b3b26699e8c930f78d18d0d0e5c contains the reviewed edition of the simulator, the book, the screenshots, the 204 infographics and the three verification programs. The SHA-256 fingerprints also make it possible to check that the main technical files haven't changed.

Campo obligatorioStatus of this revisionClosing criteria
Estado documentalChapter reviewed on 02/08/2026HTML validado e imágenes inspeccionadas
Revisión publicada14a8bf6fafa29b3b26699e8c930f78d18d0d0e5cContains all audited files

Saying a version is compatible doesn't mean the whole repository is in that state if changes remain uncommitted. Before publishing, check git status, run the tests and merge in the chapter. The identifier must point to the version that actually contains the delivered HTML, code and images. Citing an earlier commit for files that weren't yet included would break the correct trail.

Editorial status also needs to be kept separate from public status. “Validated locally” means a specific copy has passed the automated and visual tests. “Published” means the website is serving exactly those same files. The GitHub Pages address on its own doesn't prove which commit is published unless the deployment process is recorded.

The record for a new edition includes the changes added, the sources updated, the infographics replaced, the errors fixed and the sections still pending. “Sources updated” must state the documents and their editions. “Error fixed” must explain what happened before, what happens now, and which test checks it. If an image is replaced, the reason is noted: text that's hard to read, an incorrect concept, a change of source, or an editorial improvement.

Pending sections shouldn't be hidden just to call an edition “complete”. They're listed together with the person responsible, the condition needed to close them, and their dependencies. If there's no responsible person yet, that must be said too.

Pre-class check. The instructor notes the URL, commit or fingerprints, and the date, opens a scenario and checks that each control’s name matches the manual. If the public site has changed, they keep the reviewed copy or repeat the acceptance process before teaching.
Identity. Git calculates its identifiers from the objects and the content. A modification creates a different state. A documented version must link the commit, the published files and the test results. A date doesn’t distinguish between two changes made on the same day.
16.14

Documentation changelog

Linking the manual, the simulator, the date and the changes stops an explanation ending up tied to the wrong version. The changelog turns that tracking into a clear list of real changes.

What changes should be recorded, and how do you avoid inventing a history out of the current state?

The changelog is a record of changes written for people. Git keeps the modifications line by line. The changelog explains what they mean for someone using the project. As of the date of this review, no published CHANGELOG.md file was found. So no earlier history is invented. This section proposes the format for future editions and for the record of the current chapter.

Each entry includes the actual publication date, the version or commit, the category, the affected item, the effect on the reader, the source or issue, and the evidence. The useful categories are Added, Changed, Fixed, Removed, Sources, Licence and Images. A phrase like “Various improvements” doesn't explain the effect and can't be assessed.

A new feature must explain the control and where it appears. A changed parameter must state the previous value, the new one, the unit and the reason. A fixed formula must include the case that used to fail and the test it now passes. A regulatory change must identify the body, the edition, the article and the affected sections. If a screenshot or infographic is updated, it must explain whether the interface changed, the evidence used changed, or only the visual composition changed. A licence change must accurately reproduce the canonical file and receive legal review when necessary.

The record for this production describes the finished integration as a documented edition, without inventing a release number: “02/08/2026. Full book incorporated across 17 chapters and 204 units. Includes visible sources, glossary, FAQ, document control, and a dedicated PNG for each subsection. The HTML structure, navigation and references have been reviewed. The schemes, internal ballistics and the book have been checked using scripts/verify-schemes.js, scripts/verify-ballistics.js and scripts/verify-manual.js. The technical review is fixed at commit 14a8bf6….” This entry reports on work that has been checked and the tests carried out. It's not equivalent to an external certification of the physical model.

Once a versioning policy exists, the Unreleased section, or “not yet published”, will gather the changes up to the next release. When the edition closes, it will be replaced by a date and a version, with a link to the comparison. The changelog doesn't copy every commit, doesn't attribute authorship by guesswork, and doesn't hide inconvenient corrections. Showing a correction increases trust, because it lets you know which information became outdated.

Dates come from the act of publication or from the commit, never from the moment someone recalls having made the change.

Example entry. “Fixed: the slow-motion FAQ clarifies that 1.00x is normal-speed time. Affects 16.10. Evidence: loop() multiplies realDt by params.slow. Test: comparing duration at 0.50x and 1.00x.”
Granularity. A commit is a unit of history; a changelog entry is a unit of impact. Many can map to one, but the link to the diff must allow the summary to be audited.
16.15

Credits

A good changelog explains the changes that can be observed and the sources affected, without inventing dates or past states. The credits complete the picture with authorship, tools and verified contributions.

Who is responsible for the project, what contributions are on record, and how are tools, sources and reviews credited without inventing collaborators?

Original author, concept and project. Plato was built by Roberto Canales Mora. The canonical notice identifies “Copyright (c) 2026 Roberto Canales Mora” and the original URL https://robertocanales.com/proyectos#plato. The public edition of the book and the simulator can be viewed at https://rcanalescoder.github.io/Plato/.

Guidance and narrative material. The educational questions, the examples for beginners and the observations about La Bastida come from the author's personal experience. So do the repeated clays, the early frustration, the word “trillos” and part of the ground vocabulary. When they aren't rules or measurements, the manual presents them in their own boxes. Editing these memories doesn't turn them into universal data.

Assisted production. The research, the organised writing and the fifteen generative infographics for chapter 16 were produced with OpenAI Codex, following the project's editorial instructions. Each image is an original composition created for its section. It doesn't copy the figures from the linked articles or regulations. Claims are checked against the visible sources and the code, not against the tool's supposed authority.

Technologies. The simulator uses HTML, CSS, JavaScript and Canvas 2D; the book uses HTML, CSS, JavaScript and PNG. Git and GitHub maintain history and publishing; GitHub Pages serves the static site. The ISSF, FITASC, BOE, Guardia Civil, academic authors and manufacturers cited are sources, not sponsors or reviewers of Plato.

Contributors and review. As of 2 August 2026, the material reviewed contained no verifiable list of technical reviewers, teachers, clubs or contributors who had approved this chapter. That's why validation isn't attributed to La Bastida, to a federation, or to the people cited in the studies. When someone reviews an edition, their name, role, scope, date and version will be recorded. The word “reviewer” won't mean that person approves the whole project.

Editorial contract implemented. The reviewed edition has a recognisable cover, an explanatory subtitle and a real screenshot of the simulator in the header. It also includes a safety notice and one about its educational purpose. It offers a full table of contents, text search, buttons to open app.html, and side navigation through chapters and sections. Chapters can be expanded, and the active section is highlighted. Each section ends with references. There's also a cost calculator and a layout that adapts, via CSS, to desktop, tablet and mobile. The stylesheet includes a version for printing from the browser. All these features can be checked in the published files. They aren't presented as future work or as external contributions.

Needs met in a different way. The book doesn't split reading by type of user, and it doesn't offer tabs called “Simple explanation” and “Technical view”. It proposes continuous reading and separates those levels through the main text, field notes and technical notes. It doesn't show a reading percentage. Instead, it highlights the visible section in the navigation. The glossary is a chapter you can consult, not a tooltip that appears on hover. Formulae are explained within the text and in technical notes, not in separate panels. Scenarios link to the simulator, but they don't yet include their parameters in the address. Permanent buttons let you return to the application. The printable version is obtained using the browser's own function, not through a separate PDF.

Features this edition doesn't claim to offer. There's no dark mode switch and no dedicated viewer for zooming into the infographics. Nor are there interactive hotspots on the screenshots, definitions that pop up when you hover over a word, links that automatically rebuild a scenario, or a numeric progress indicator. It isn't claimed that a formal accessibility audit exists, or full compatibility with every device. These features may be considered in future versions. Stating this here stops the table of contents being mistaken for a list of features that already exist.

How to cite. “Canales Mora, Roberto. Plato: web book and clay target shooting simulator. Version or commit consulted, date accessed, https://robertocanales.com/proyectos#plato”. If you cite a modified version, also identify who modified it and link to the original.
Documentary CRediT. Roles are granted with evidence: project authorship, narrative contribution, assisted writing, visual generation, review and maintenance are all distinct contributions. A tool is credited as a tool; it doesn’t receive editorial responsibility or scientific authority.

What we have learned

  • Every claim must use a source competent in its own field and state its edition, scope and limit.
  • Spanish regulations, ISSF and FITASC rules, physical evidence, field experience and Plato's code are separate layers.
  • Trust isn't expressed with invented percentages. It's explained by stating where the data comes from, how it was obtained, how long it's valid for, and how it can be checked.
  • A commit or fingerprint links the manual and the simulator; the review date doesn't replace an identifiable version.
  • The glossary and FAQ provide guidance, but they don't replace authorisation, training, diagnosis, regulation or a real test.
  • A changelog describes verified impact and doesn't reconstruct dates or releases that aren't on record.
  • Roberto Canales Mora is the original author of Plato, and the attribution keeps the project's original URL.

Chapter 17

Useful conclusions from the book

This manual doesn't turn anyone into a shooter, but it offers an ordered way of watching. Breaking shooting down into parts helps you understand it; putting them back together lets you practise with purpose. Safety always comes before results.

This journey has brought together responsible access, the shooting ground, the gun, the cartridge, mistakes, geometry, physics and disciplines. Physics explains the sport, but it doesn't replace physical learning or real experience.

The first conclusion: you don't aim at the clay like a fixed target

The clay keeps changing position while the shooter sees it, starts to move, pulls the trigger, and waits for the pellets to cover the distance. Because of this, a picture that looks right at the moment of firing can already be too late. Leading doesn't mean always applying a fixed distance. Nor does it mean losing sight of the clay. It means keeping a continuous movement going, so that the clay's flight path and the pellets' path meet at the same place and at the same moment. That meeting is shaped by the direction and side of the flight, the distance, the cartridge's velocity and how long the shooter takes to react.

For this reason, corrections should be explained in terms of the clay's actual movement. “Right” and “left” only indicate a side of the screen. “Ahead” and “behind”, on the other hand, explain where the shot landed relative to the flight, and they swap sides when the clay changes direction. What's more, a shot can have the right lead and still fly high. It can also have the right height and still be too late. Separating the horizontal correction from the vertical one lets you work on one mistake at a time without disturbing the whole movement.

The second: the pattern is a distribution, not a single projectile

Meeting the clay doesn't depend on a single projectile. The cartridge fires many pellets. These don't form a rigid disc, and they don't all travel in one perfect plane. The cloud spreads out, loses speed and also stretches out in depth. The choke influences that spread, but it doesn't produce exactly the same pattern with every shot. The result depends on the gun, the barrel, the cartridge, the wad, the pellet size, the distance and the small variations in every shot. This variation doesn't contradict the physics. It simply means it has to be explained without promising a precision that doesn't exist.

The simulator represents the pellets one by one. It also lets you view a plane set almost perpendicular to the shot's direction. This view separates two things: where the centre of the pattern was aimed, and the specific hits that appeared around it. This avoids a false promise. Placing the centre in the right area increases the chance of breaking the clay, but it doesn't guarantee a hit on every attempt. In real practice, the decisive check remains the pattern board, using the actual gun and ammunition you're going to use.

The third: a discipline is a measured system

The geometry of the meeting point and the pattern always belong to a specific discipline. Olympic Trap, Universal Trench and a robotic machine aren't three names for the same type of throw. The position of the machines changes, along with the schemes, the permitted angles, the height, the landing distance and the rhythm. How the shooter reads the release also changes. That's why the book distinguishes regulated conditions from popular or experimental setups. A name can't replace a measurement.

For that same reason, Plato shows its control parameters and runs automatic tests on its schemes. If a release is claimed to meet a rule, the specific edition of the regulation and the measurements checked must be stated. If a Robot setting deliberately aims for an extreme clay, it must be labelled non-normative. Being able to trace the origin of every piece of data matters especially when the project is presented to a federation. An attractive picture must never hide where a figure comes from, which version it belongs to, or what margin it carries.

The fourth: a simulator teaches relationships, not the full sensation

Once those measurements are defined, the simulator lets you observe them more calmly. Slow motion keeps the model's physical relationships intact, but gives you more time to see what's happening on screen. Users can repeat a release, hide the aid, change the wind, compare chokes or review two shots. This laboratory makes it possible to isolate variables that, during a real round, change very quickly and are hard to remember. It also helps you ask the instructor more specific questions. Instead of just saying “why did I miss?”, the shooter can ask “did I stop my swing?”, “was the centre too low?” or “what changes if the second pattern is tighter?”.

Even so, a screen doesn't reproduce the gun's weight or balance. Nor does it reproduce recoil, sound, the pressure of your turn, fatigue, eye dominance or the three-dimensional fit of a stock. The simulator doesn't authorise you to handle firearms, doesn't grant licences and doesn't replace a certified ground. Its educational value increases when these limits are explained clearly. A model that states what it represents and what it leaves out is more useful than one that seems to know everything.

The fifth: learning means closing a loop

These observations only turn into learning once a simple loop is completed: watch, propose a cause, change a single variable and check the result. In the simulator, you can repeat the same clay with guidance and without it. On the ground, you can work on a single point over a short round and review it with an instructor. Changing the hold, the stock, the choke, the speed, the stance and the technique all at once creates a lot of activity, but teaches you little about what actually caused the result. Isolating variables is useful both for beginners and for anyone reviewing the equations.

Learning also has an emotional side. Missing doesn't prove someone is incapable. It shows that a relationship of timing or space hasn't settled yet. A specific correction is easier to take in than a general judgement about the shooter. Resting, receiving less information at once and keeping a safe routine all help you keep learning for longer. No score justifies training through pain, extreme tiredness or a lack of attention.

What each reader should take away

Anyone who has never shot before should finish the book with enough words to walk into a club without having to fake experience. They should also recognise that safety is the first priority, and understand why the shot has to lead the clay. Anyone who already shoots should find a way to organise their mistakes, compare setups and talk about their results more precisely. A technically minded reader should be able to follow the model's assumptions, coordinates, sources and tests. That way, they can tell the educational simplifications apart and propose improvements that other people can check.

The project can improve its aerodynamics, its pattern, its visual tuning and its real-world validation. Every improvement must answer a useful question, state where its data comes from, and help understanding. Drawing more isn't enough.

The essentials, in seven ideas

  1. Safety and in-person training come before any technique.
  2. Clay target shooting is an interception problem: you have to think in terms of a future position.
  3. Height and lead are different corrections, and must be read according to the direction of flight.
  4. The pattern is variable, three-dimensional and dependent on the gun, cartridge, choke and distance.
  5. Disciplines are validated against specific rules and metrics, not against appearances.
  6. The simulator helps you observe and repeat, but the shooting ground and the instructor provide the real check.
  7. Learning better means changing one variable, measuring the result, and keeping what works.
Technical close. Plato doesn’t need to claim it reproduces the whole of reality to be credible. Its credibility depends on its states, units, equations, timescales, sources and tests being open to review. It also depends on the visual aids using the same physical state that decides the result. Finally, any difference between the model, the regulations and what’s observed on the ground must be logged as an open task.
